e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}\,\big(\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}-\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\big)=e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}\,\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\equiv\frac{\delta S^{\rm red}[\,\phi\,]}{\delta\phi^{i}},\;\quad\;e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}\equiv\frac{\delta e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}(\phi)}{\delta\phi^{i}},

(3.8)

where we took into account that in view of (
3.6
) the covector
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
is orthogonal to the surface of constraints
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}=0
,

\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{i}}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\big(\varphi(\phi)\big)\,=\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\,\big|_{\,\theta=0}\,e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}\,=\,0.

(3.9)

An important question is the relation of the original
parental
theory and the
restricted
one from the point of view their physical equivalence. Solutions of the system of equations (
3.5
) are obviously inequivalent to those of the parental theory,
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}=0
, when on shell the Lagrange multipliers
\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
are nonvanishing. Note that in view of linearity of the
\lambda
-action (
3.4
) in
\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
the variational equations with respect to Lagrange multipliers do not allow one to express them from the full set of equations of motion. There is another way to write down their equations of motion by contracting the first set of equations (
3.5
) with the parental theory generators
\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}
and use the Noether identities (
3.1
). Then we get

\displaystyle\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}\,=\,0,

(3.10)

where
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}=Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}(\varphi)
is the
gauge-restriction operator
which will be very important in what follows,

Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}\,\equiv\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}\,}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\alpha}.

(3.11)

This equation for
\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
has a unique solution
\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}=0
only when the rank of
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}
is maximal, that is it coincides with the range of the index
{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
enumerating the constraint functions
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
. In this case the meaning of the constraints
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}(\varphi)=0
is nothing but a partial gauge fixing of gauge invariance of the parental theory (
{\rm range}\>{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}=m_{1}<m_{0}={\rm range}\>\alpha
).

On the contrary, when the rank of gauge-restriction operator
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}
is lower than
m_{1}

\displaystyle\rank Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}\,=\,m_{1}-m_{2}

(3.12)

then
m_{2}
Lagrange multipliers can be freely specified, the rest
m_{1}-m_{2}
of them being fixed as unique functions of the former free ones. This implies, in particular, the existence of
m_{2}
gauge invariants of the parental theory constrained to be zero in restricted theory. To see this, note that the rank deficiency implies that operator
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}
has
m_{2}
left zero vectors
Y^{\!{\mathchoice{A}{A}{\scriptscriptstyle{A}}{A}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
,

Y^{\!{\mathchoice{A}{A}{\scriptscriptstyle{A}}{A}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\,}Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}=0,\;\quad\;{\rm range}\>{\mathchoice{A}{A}{\scriptscriptstyle{A}}{A}}=m_{2}\leq m_{1},

(3.13)

and this, according to the definition of this operator, implies the existence of
m_{2}
parental gauge invariants

\theta^{\mathchoice{A}{A}{\scriptscriptstyle{A}}{A}}(\varphi)\equiv Y^{\!{\mathchoice{A}{A}{\scriptscriptstyle{A}}{A}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}(\varphi)\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}(\varphi),\;\quad\;\theta^{\mathchoice{A}{A}{\scriptscriptstyle{A}}{A}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}\,}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\alpha}\big|_{\,\theta=0}=0,

(3.14)

which are constrained to vanish. So generically the restricted theory (
3.4
) is
inequivalent
to the parental gauge theory
\hat{S}[\,\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}\,]
, because the latter does not
a priori
impose any restrictions on its gauge-invariant objects. In what follows we will consider such restricted theories which incorporate new physics beyond their parental ones.

There is another representation of the solution for Lagrange multipliers. If one constructs the set
\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}
dual to covariant vectors
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
,

\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\,\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}\,=\,\delta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}},

(3.15)

then, contracting the equation of motion (
3.5
) with
\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}
one obtains

\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}=\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}.

(3.16)

For local in spacetime restriction functions
4
4

4

We consider either ultralocal (algebraic) restriction functions
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}(\varphi)
or those for which
m_{1}(={\rm range}\>{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}})
ultralocal independent combinations of
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
can be expressed from the restriction conditions
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}(\varphi)=0
. In such a case a useful criterion for the existence of local (in fact ultralocal) vectors
\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}
dual to
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
, (
3.15
) is the existence of the ultralocal nondegenerate linear combination
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}c^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}
with local vectors
c^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}
(in particular, the presence of the ultralocal minor of maximal
m_{1}
rank in the matrix
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
).

\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
this is a local expression for Lagrange multipliers in terms of
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
— the left-hand side of equations of motion of the
parental
theory. Resolving Lagrange multipliers
\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
in restricted equations of motion (
3.5
) leads to the projected set of equations,

\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}(\delta^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}-\theta^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}})=0,

(3.17)

equivalent to
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}=0
. The projector
(\delta^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}-\theta^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}})
enforces only a part of parental equations of motion
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
“tangential” to restriction surface
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}=0
. Which is equivalent to (
3.8
) and reinstates the observation that along tangential directions equations of motion for restricted theory coincides with that for parental theory.

On the contrary, projected on complementary (“normal”) directions, equations of motion for these two theories differ. Contraction of the parental equations
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}=0
and restricted theory equations
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}-\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}=0
with
\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
gives correspondingly
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}=0
and
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}=\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
. The normal subset of the parental equations in addition to equations (
3.17
) further restricts classical configurations of fields
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
in parental theory. While the normal subset of the restricted theory equations merely expresses
\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
in terms of
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
and thus does not additionally constrain the latter.
5
5

5

Comparing the sets of equations of motion which define the dynamics of
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
in parental and restricted theories one finds that in the restricted theory
m_{1}
constraints
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}(\varphi)=0
are “removed” while
m_{1}
new constraints
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}(\varphi)=0
are applied. As it is this does not, however, predict the number of degrees of freedom in restricted theory, since this requires a deeper analysis of interrelation between the restriction conditions (
3.2
) and dynamical and gauge structures of the parental theory.

However due to gauge invariance of the parental theory (
3.1
) not all dynamical restrictions on
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
in the right-hand side of (
3.16
) are effectively removed. The linear dependence
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}=0
between
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
may express a certain part of
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
in (
3.16
) as linear combinations of tangential equations (
3.17
) which are still frozen to
0
within the restricted equations of motion. Obviously such linear combinations are found by first contracting (
3.16
) with
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
and then with
\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}
which leads to
\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\alpha}=\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\alpha}
. The same structure
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\alpha}
appears in the left-hand side of tangential equations (
3.17
) after contracting with
\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}
and thus vanishes on-shell. This reinstates constraints on Lagrange multipliers (
3.10
),
\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}=0
.

III.2
Gauge symmetry and reducibility

We assume that the restricted theory does not acquire local gauge symmetries beyond those of the original parental action. On the other hand, not all gauge transformations of the the parental theory
\delta_{\xi}\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}=\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\alpha}\xi^{\alpha}
generate symmetries of the reduced action (
3.4
). These symmetries are only those which preserve the constraints

\delta_{\xi}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\,\big|_{\,\theta=0}\equiv\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\alpha}\xi^{\alpha}\,\big|_{\,\theta=0}\,=\,0,

(3.18)

so that with the definition (
3.11
) the allowed gauge transformation parameters
\xi^{\alpha}_{\rm red}
in the restricted theory should be solutions of the linear equation
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}\xi^{\alpha}_{\rm red}=0
, i.e. right zero vectors of the matrix
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}
. The subspace of reduced gauge parameters can be obtained by projecting gauge parameters of the parental theory
\xi^{\alpha}
with a projector
T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}
which is orthogonal in the space of gauge indices to
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}
,

\xi^{\alpha}_{\rm red}=T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}\,\xi^{\beta},\;\quad\;Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha\,}T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}=0.

(3.19)

On the other hand, gauge symmetries of the restricted theory can be formulated in the parental field space of
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
with free (nonreduced) gauge parameters
\xi^{\alpha}
. This happens if instead of projecting
\xi^{\alpha}
we project with respect to the gauge index
\alpha
the original parental generators
\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\alpha}
. Thus we get the
reducible
set of gauge generators

\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\beta}\,\equiv\,\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\alpha\,}T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}.

(3.20)

This formulation is covariant from the viewpoint of the parental gauge theory because the original multiplets of parental field indices
{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
and gauge indices
\alpha
remain unsplitted, but the price paid for this covariance is the reducibility of the generators
\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\beta}
. They are indeed reducible because the projector
T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}
in view of rank deficiency (following from (
3.19
)) possess right zero vectors
k^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
which become also the right zero vectors of
\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\beta}

T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta\,}k^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}=0,\;\quad\;\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\beta\,}k^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}=0.

(3.21)

Despite reducibility an important advantage of such projected representation is that it generates gauge transformations with arbitrary
unrestricted
gauge parameters
\xi^{\alpha}
. In the course of subsequent Batalin-Vilkovisky extension of configuration space it will give rise to gauge ghosts equivalent to that of the parental theory. This property is achieved at the cost of
reducibility
of the projected gauge structure (
3.20
) and introduction of ghost for ghosts fields.

The projector
T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}
may be explicitly constructed in terms of the left- and right-kernel bases (
3.19
),(
3.21
)

T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}\,=\,\delta^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}-k^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\,{(Qk)^{-1}}\vphantom{|}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,Q^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{\,\beta}\,\equiv\,T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}(Q,k).

(3.22)

Left kernel —
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}
(
3.11
) — is fixed by the restriction conditions and the choice of parental gauge generators. Thus (
3.22
) may be considered as the family parametrized by its right kernel basis
k^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
. The structure of
T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}(Q,k)
, which in what follows will be referred without specification of kernels, is analogous to the set of projectors
T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}(s,\sigma)
(
2.36
) introduced above.

Here we emphasize the property that the matrix
(Qk)^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\equiv Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\gamma}\,k^{\gamma}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
is not directly invertible. Rather it could have rank equal or lower to that of
\rank Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\gamma}=m_{1}-m_{2}
. The rank deficiency is dictated by the requirement of new physics incorporated by the restricted theory (
3.12
) — physical inequivalence to the parental gauge theory. As discussed in Appendix
A
, this extra difficulty can be circumvented by the Moore-Penrose construction of the generalized inverse
MoorePenrose
, provided the following rank restriction conditions (
3.23
) are satisfied,

\rank{(Qk)^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}}=\rank{Q^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\,\alpha}}=\rank{k^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}}.

(3.23)

This guarantees unambiguous definition of projector (
3.22
) and its correct rank property,
\rank{T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}}=m_{0}-m_{1}+m_{2}
.

Rank restriction requirements (
3.23
) in particular are guaranteed when
k^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
is parametrized in terms of nondegenerate two-forms
B^{\beta\alpha}(\varphi)
and
c_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}(\varphi)
— metrics in spaces of
\alpha
-indices and
{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
-indices respectively,

k^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}=B^{\beta\alpha}Q^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\,\alpha}c_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}},\,\quad\,T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}=\delta^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}-B^{\alpha\gamma}Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\gamma}\big(\,QBQ^{T}\,\big)^{-1}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{\,\beta},

(3.24)

which will be used in unimodular gravity calculations in Sec.
V
. In fact in this case the projector
T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}
is defined by
Q^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\,\alpha}
and
B^{\beta\alpha}
only because the dependence on
c_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
completely cancels out.
6
6

6

The “symmetric” form (
3.24
) has the structure analogous to that of symmetric projector
\varPi_{\alpha\beta}
(
2.45
) (with the left index raised) when
\varkappa_{\alpha\beta}\,\leftrightarrow\,(B^{\beta\alpha})^{-1}
and
\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,\leftrightarrow\,B^{\alpha\beta}Q^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\,\beta}c_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
.

The generators (
3.20
) of gauge transformations in restricted theory
7
7

7

In this section we focus on the gauge transformations (
3.20
) of fields
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
in the restricted theory (
3.4
). However, the configuration space of the latter also contains fields
\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
. The Noether identities for the equations of motion (
3.5
) show that the gauge transformation of Lagrange multipliers should be zero,
\delta_{\xi}\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\equiv\mathcal{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\alpha}\xi^{\alpha}=0
. This is so when the projector
T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}(Q,k)
(
3.22
) in the gauge generators (
3.20
) is constructed with respect to the operator
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}
(
3.11
) in the whole neighborhood of the restriction surface
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}(\varphi)=0
. This, in particular, implies that
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}
has a constant rank in this neighborhood. We assumed this property and so the gauge symmetry representation
\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\beta}=\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\alpha\,}T^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}(Q,k)
(
3.20
) and
\mathcal{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\alpha}=0
is enough for the purpose of this paper. In generic case when one uses the projector (
3.22
) with some other
constant rank
left kernel,
Q^{\prime{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\,\alpha}=Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}+\Gamma^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}\alpha}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}
, differing from
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}
(
3.11
) off the restriction surface, Noether identities imply nonzero generators
\mathcal{R}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}\alpha}=\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\Gamma^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}\beta}T^{\beta}_{\,\alpha}(Q^{\prime},k)
. This is inevitable when the rank of
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}
(
3.11
) jumps outside of the restriction surface.

are thus far defined in the parental theory field space of
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
. They determine the gauge transformations
\delta_{\xi}^{\rm red}\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}=\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\beta}\xi^{\beta}
tangential to the surface of
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}(\varphi)=0
. As any field on this surface can be parametrized by
\phi^{i}
these gauge transformations can be expressed via the gauge transformations of the
reduced restricted
gauge theory
\delta_{\xi}^{\rm red}\phi^{i}=\mathcal{R}^{i}_{\beta}\xi^{\beta}
,
\delta_{\xi}^{\rm red}\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}=e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}\,\delta_{\xi}^{\rm red}\phi^{i}
, so that the corresponding generators
\mathcal{R}^{i}_{\beta}
of reduced space gauge fields
\phi^{i}
are related to
\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\beta}
by obvious pushforward relations

\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\beta}(e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}(\phi))\,=\,\frac{\delta e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}(\phi)}{\delta\phi^{i}}\mathcal{R}^{{i}}_{\beta}(\phi^{i})\,\equiv\,e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}\mathcal{R}^{{i}}_{\beta}.

(3.25)

Thus, the reduced-space representation of the restricted gauge theory has reducible generators
\mathcal{R}^{{i}}_{\beta}
and their first-stage reducibility generators
\mathcal{Z}^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
,

\frac{\delta S^{\rm red}[\,\phi\,]}{\delta\phi^{i}}\,\mathcal{R}^{{i}}_{\beta}(\phi)=0,\;\quad\;\mathcal{R}^{{i}}_{\beta\,}\mathcal{Z}^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}=0

(3.26)

where
\mathcal{Z}^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
exhaust linear combinations of right zero vectors of the projector
T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}
and have the form

\mathcal{Z}^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}=k^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}\,}\mu^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}

(3.27)

with arbitrary factor
\mu^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
, so that
\rank{k^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\mu^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}}=\rank{k^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}}
. Such natural choice imply off-shell first-stage reducibility (
3.26
) of projected gauge generators.

Note that the projectors (
3.22
) generically are nonlocal depending on structure of restriction functions and gauge generators involved in the construction of gauge-restriction operator
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}
.

III.3
Gauge algebra of a restricted theory

To proceed further we have to know the gauge algebra of the restricted theory generators. The
a priori
maximum that can be stated from the first of their relations (
3.26
) (under certain assumptions of regularity of
\mathcal{R}^{{i}}_{\beta}(\phi)

BV3
) is that they satisfy an open algebra (
2.3
) with some structure functions and
S_{,{i}}
replaced by
S^{\rm red}_{,{i}}
. Quite remarkably, we will be able to derive this algebra first by expressing via Eq. (
3.25
)
\mathcal{R}^{{i}}_{\beta}
in terms of
\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\beta}
and using the algebra of the projected (full space) generators (
3.20
),
\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\beta}=\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\alpha}T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}
. The latter, in its turn, follows from the gauge algebra of the parental theory and turns out to be also closed in the case of closure of the algebra of
\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}
. Thus, finally we will show that the resulting algebra is also closed as long as we start with the closed algebra of the parental theory.

Expressing
\mathcal{R}^{{i}}_{\beta}
in terms of
\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\beta}
goes with the aid of covariant vectors
e^{i}_{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
dual to
e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{j}}

\mathcal{R}^{{i}}_{\alpha}(\phi)\,=\,e^{i}_{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}\big(e(\phi)\big).

(3.28)

These vectors satisfy the biorthogonality relation
e^{i}_{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}\,e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{j}}=\delta^{i}_{\,{j}}

and represent a part of the complete local basis
(e^{i}_{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}},\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}})
in the cotangent bundle of the manifold of
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
. Vectors
e^{i}_{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
may be chosen orthogonal to the set
\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}
dual to
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
(
3.15
), so that
(e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}},\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}})
and
(e^{i}_{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}},\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}})
form biorthogonal pair of tangent and cotangent bases

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pte^{i}_{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}\,e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{j}}=\delta^{i}_{\,{j}},\;\;\;\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\,\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}=\delta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}},\;\;\;e^{i}_{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}\,\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}=0,\;\;\;\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}\,e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{j}}=0,

(3.29)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pte^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}\,e^{i}_{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}+\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}=\delta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}},

(3.30)

the last equation here expressing completeness of this basis.
8
8

8

A particular “symmetric” biorthogonal basis may be constructed with the aid of the nondegenerate metric
G_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
on the manifold of
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
. This induces the metric
G_{{i}{j}}\equiv e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}\,G_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\,e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{j}}
,
G^{{i}{j}}\equiv(G_{{j}{i}})^{-1}
, on the reduced
\phi^{i}
-space — the surface of
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}(\varphi)=0
, and its inverse
G^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\equiv(G_{{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}})^{-1}
induces the metric in the directions normal to this surface,
G^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\equiv\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\,G^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
,

G_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\equiv(G^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}})^{-1}
.
Starting from mutually orthogonal
e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}
and
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
one can construct complementary basis elements
e^{i}_{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
and
\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
satisfying (
3.29
),(
3.30
) as

e^{i}_{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}=G^{{i}{j}}e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{j}}G_{{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
and

\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}=G^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}G_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
.

For fixed
e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{j}}
and
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
there is a freedom of choosing complementary basis elements

e^{{i}}_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\,\to\,e^{{i}}_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}+b^{i}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}},\;\,\quad\;\,\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\,\to\,\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}-e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}b^{i}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}

(3.31)

which preserves (
3.29
),(
3.30
). However this freedom does not spoil expression (
3.28
) since the shift ambiguity term in
e^{{i}}_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
is killed due to defining property of restricted generators, (
3.20
)
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\beta}=\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\alpha\,}T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}=Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}=0
, thus making expression (
3.28
) for
\mathcal{R}^{{i}}_{\alpha}(\phi)
unambiguous.

Using (
3.28
) we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{{i}}_{[\,\alpha,\,{j}}\mathcal{R}^{j}_{\beta\,]}

\displaystyle\;=\;

\displaystyle e^{i}_{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}\,\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{[\,\alpha,\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\beta\,]}+\mathcal{R}^{k}_{[\,\alpha}\,\mathcal{R}^{j}_{\beta\,]}e^{i}_{{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}},{j}}\,e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{k}}

(3.32)

\displaystyle\;=\;

\displaystyle e^{i}_{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}\,\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{[\,\alpha,\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\beta\,]},

where square brackets denote pairs of antisymmetrized indices and the second term in the middle part vanishes because for dual
e^{i}_{{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
and
e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{k}}
(
3.29
)
e^{i}_{{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}},{j}}e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{k}}=-e^{i}_{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{k}{j}}\equiv-e^{i}_{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}\,\delta^{2}e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}(\phi)/\delta\phi^{k}\delta\phi^{j}
and so antisymmetrization in indices kills it. Thus the gauge algebra of
\mathcal{R}^{i}_{\alpha}
directly follows from that of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}
.

As shown in Appendix
B
the commutator of projected generators
\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}
replicates the algebraic relation of the parental theory. If the latter is prescribed by

\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\beta}\,-\,\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\beta,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\alpha}\;=\;\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma}\hat{C}^{\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}+\hat{E}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\alpha\beta}\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}},

(3.33)

then the projected generators of the restricted gauge theory
\mathcal{R}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\beta}=\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\alpha}T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}
, which are defined by Eqs. (
3.20
)–(
3.22
) both on and
outside
of the constraint surface
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}=0
, are subject to a similar relation

\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\beta}\,-\,\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\beta,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\alpha}\;=\;\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma}C^{\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}+{E}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\alpha\beta}\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}

(3.34)

with new structure functions
C^{\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}
and
{E}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle{C}^{\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle T^{\gamma}_{\>\zeta}\hat{C}^{\zeta}_{{\,\delta}\epsilon}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\alpha}T^{\epsilon}_{\>\beta}+N^{\gamma}_{\,\beta\alpha}-N^{\gamma}_{\,\alpha\beta},

(3.35)

\displaystyle{E}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}}D^{{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{L}{L}{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}{L}}}\hat{E}^{{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}{\mathchoice{L}{L}{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}{L}}}_{\gamma{\,\delta}}\,T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta},

(3.36)

where

\displaystyle N^{\gamma}_{\,\alpha\beta}\!

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle T^{\gamma}_{\>{\,\delta}}k^{{\,\delta}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}},{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}{(Qk)^{-1}}\vphantom{|}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}Q^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{\,\alpha}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\,\beta},

(3.37)

\displaystyle D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\,

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\delta^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}-\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\,\epsilon}k^{\epsilon}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}{(Qk)^{-1}}\vphantom{|}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\theta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}.

(3.38)

Thus, in view of (
3.32
) for a
closed
algebra of the parental theory with
\hat{E}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\alpha\beta}=0
one gets a
closed
gauge algebra of reduced fields representation of the restricted theory with
{E}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\alpha\beta}=0
and the same structure functions (
3.35
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{i}_{\alpha,{j}}\mathcal{R}^{j}_{\beta}\,-\,\mathcal{R}^{i}_{\beta,{j}}\mathcal{R}^{j}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{i}_{\gamma}C^{\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}.

(3.39)

These structure functions can be nonlocal and with respect to its lower indices have both transversal and longitudinal nature regarding the projector
T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}
because of the properties of
N^{\gamma}_{\,\alpha\beta}
-tail of the expression (
3.35
),

N^{\gamma}_{\,\alpha\beta}=T^{\gamma}_{\>{\,\delta}}N^{{\,\delta}}_{\,\alpha\beta}=N^{\gamma}_{\,\alpha{\,\delta}}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}
and
N^{\gamma}_{\,{\,\delta}\beta}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\alpha}=0
.

IV
One-loop effective action of restricted gauge theory

IV.1
Two representations of the one-loop effective action

The reduced representation of the restricted gauge theory, which was constructed above, is subject to the BV formalism of the first-stage reducible model with first-stage reducibility generators
\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\propto k^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
. Therefore, one can use Eq. (
2.49
) or Eq. (
II.4
) along with Eqs. (
2.50
)–(
2.52
) and the replacement of
S[\,\phi\,]
by
S^{\rm red}[\,\phi\,]
in order to build its one-loop effective action. However, as it was discussed above, our goal is to perform quantization in the representation of the original parental fields
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
, rather than in terms of the reduced variables
\phi^{i}
. Hence, an interesting task arises — to convert these algorithms into this representation.

Such a conversion is, of course, based on the relations of embedding the restricted theory into the parental one (
3.6
)–(
3.7
) and on the classical equation of motion (
3.5
) which determines the background on top of which the semiclassical expansion is built. These relations imply that all field-dependent entities of the restricted theory, including all gauge-fixing elements, result from the embedding of
\phi^{i}
into the space of
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
. Conversely, the objects of the restricted theory are the functions on the parental configuration space. Therefore, like the relation (
3.7
) for the classical parental and reduced actions
S^{\rm red}[\,\phi\,]=\hat{S}[\,e(\phi)\,]
, we have

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\chi^{\alpha},\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}},\omega^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\,\alpha},\varkappa^{\alpha\beta},\rho^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\;=\;\chi^{\alpha}(e(\phi)),\,\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}(e(\phi)),\,\omega^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\,\alpha}(e(\phi)),\,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;\;\;\varkappa^{\alpha\beta}(e(\phi)),\,\rho^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}(e(\phi)),

(4.1)

where, to avoid messy notation, we do not supply
\chi^{\alpha}(\varphi)
and other gauge-fixing quantities of the parental theory by hats. In particular, this means that the gauge condition matrices
X^{\alpha}_{,{i}}
, (
2.53
), and
X^{\alpha}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
in both representations are related by the embedding formula for a covector,

X^{\alpha}_{,{i}}=e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}X^{\alpha}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}},\quad X^{\alpha}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}=\chi^{\alpha}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}-\sigma^{\alpha}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{c}}},{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\,({s}\sigma)^{-1\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\;\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,{s}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{\,\beta}\,\chi^{\beta}.

(4.2)

The relation between
S^{\rm red}_{,{i}{j}}
and
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
is trickier. From (
3.7
) it follows that on shell

\displaystyle S^{\rm red}_{,{i}{j}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{j}}\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}+\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}{j}}\big)\big|_{\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}=0}

(4.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{j}}\big(\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}-\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\big)\big|_{\,S^{\lambda}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}=0,\;\;\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}=0}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{j}}S^{\lambda}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\,\big|_{\,S^{\lambda}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}=0,\;\;\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}=0}\,,

where we took into account the equation of motion
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}=\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
and the corollary of Eq. (
3.6
)
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{j}}+\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}{j}}=0
. Therefore, with the inclusion of the gauge-breaking term we have for the operator (
2.50
)

F_{{i}{j}}\,=\,e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{j}}F_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}},\;\quad\;F_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\,=\,\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\!-\!\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\!-\!X^{\alpha}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\varPi_{\alpha\beta}X^{\beta}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}.

(4.4)

What remains now is to convert the functional determinant of
F_{{i}{j}}
on the space of
\phi^{i}
to the functional determinant of
F_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
on the space of
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
. This can be done by comparing two expressions for one and the same Gaussian integral with the quadratic part of the action
S^{\lambda}[\,\varphi,\lambda\,]
in the exponential. On the one hand it equals

\displaystyle\int Dh\,D\lambda\;e^{\,i\left(\frac{1}{2}\,h^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}F_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}h^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}-\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}h^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\left(\det\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c}F_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}&-\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\\
\hline\cr-\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}&0\end{array}\right]\right)^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\big(\!\det F_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\big)^{-1/2}\,\big(\!\det\varTheta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\big)^{-1/2},

(4.8)

\displaystyle\varTheta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,\equiv\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}F^{-1\,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}},\quad\quad

(4.9)

where
h^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
denotes perturbations of
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
. On the other hand, this integral can be calculated in the parametrization of reduced fields
\phi^{i}
with perturbations
h^{i}
and specially chosen set of remaining “reducibility” fields
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
with perturbations
h^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
,

h^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}=e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}h^{i}_{(e)}+\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}h^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{(\theta)},\;\quad\;h^{i}_{(e)}=e^{i}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}h^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}},\;\quad\;h^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{(\theta)}=\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}h^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}},

(4.10)

where
e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}
,
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
,
e^{i}_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
,
\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
are calculated on the classical background and chosen to satisfy the biorthogonality relations (
3.29
),(
3.30
). Thus, making the change of integration variables
h^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}\to(h^{i}_{(e)},h^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{(\theta)})
we have for the same integral

\displaystyle\int Dh\;e^{\,i\,\frac{1}{2}h^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}F_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}h^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\delta(\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}h^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}})

\displaystyle\qquad=\;\det\big[\,e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}\,\,\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\,\big]\int Dh_{(e)}\,Dh_{(\theta)}\;e^{\,i\,\frac{1}{2}h^{i}_{(e)}F_{{i}{j}}h^{j}_{(e)}}\delta(h^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{(\theta)})

\displaystyle\qquad=\;\det\big[\,e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}\,\,\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\,\big]\,\big(\!\det F_{{i}{j}}\big)^{-1/2},

(4.11)

whence the comparison of these two expressions gives

\det F_{{i}{j}}\,=\,\det F_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\,\det\varTheta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,\big(\!\det\big[\,e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}\,\,\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\,\big]\big)^{2}.

(4.12)

For ultralocal restriction constraint (
3.2
) the reparametrization (
4.10
) is also ultralocal in spacetime,
e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}},\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}},e^{i}_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}},\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\sim\delta(x,y)
, and the last squared determinant here contributes to the effective action
\delta(0)
terms. Repeating the derivation procedure of Eqs. (
4.10
)–(
4.12
) with
F_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
replaced by some ultralocal symmetric matrix
G_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
proportional to undifferentiated delta function of spacetime coordinates — the metric on the configuration space of
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
,

G_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\sim\delta(x_{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}},x_{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}),

(4.13)

one finds the expression for Jacobian
\big[\,e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}\,\,\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\,\big]
in the right-hand side of (
4.12
) in terms of the corresponding functional determinants of this metric, its inverse
G^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\equiv(G_{{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}})^{-1}
, the induced metric
G_{{i}{j}}
of the reduced
\phi^{i}
-space and the metric
G_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
in the
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
-directions (see footnote
8
)

\displaystyle G_{{i}{j}}\equiv e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}\,G_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\,e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{j}},\;\quad\;G^{{i}{j}}\equiv(G_{{j}{i}})^{-1},

\displaystyle G^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\equiv\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\,G^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}},\;\quad\;G_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\equiv(G^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}})^{-1}.

(4.14)

The square of this Jacobian therefore reads

\big(\!\det\big[\,e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}\,\,\theta^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\,\big]\big)^{2}\,=\,\frac{\det G_{{i}{j}}}{\det G_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}}\,\det G_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,\sim\,\exp\big(\delta(0)(...)\big).

(4.15)

Note that
G^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
is ultralocal (for ultralocal restrictions) in contrast to the operator
\varTheta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
defined by Eq. (
4.9
)

Thus, this is an inessential normalization factor of the generating functional or, if treated seriously, it can be absorbed into the definition of the local path integral measure provided one identifies the ultralocal matrix
G_{{i}{j}}
with the Hessian of the gauge-fixed reduced action
S^{\rm red}_{\rm gf}[\,\phi\,]
with respect to the configuration space velocities
\dot{\phi}
,
9
9

9

Here, of course, the functional dependence of the reduced and parental actions on velocities is mediated by their gauge-fixed Lagrangians, i.e.
S^{\rm red}_{\rm gf}=\int dt\,L^{\rm red}_{\rm gf}(\phi,\dot{\phi})
and
S^{\lambda}_{\rm gf}=\int dt\,L^{\lambda}_{\rm gf}(\varphi,\dot{\varphi})
. The gauge-fixed action is just the corresponding classical action with added gauge breaking term. Also remember that we consider relativistic gauges, so that the “kinetic” metric (
IV.1
) in reduced theory is nondegenerate. Extending
e^{i}_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}G_{{i}{j}}e^{j}_{{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
with linear combinations
b_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}+\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}b_{{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
one acquires the nondegenerate metric
G_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
.

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\int D\phi\,\,\to\,\,\int D\phi\,\big(\!\det G_{{i}{j}}\big)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptG_{{i}{j}}=\frac{\delta^{2}S^{\rm red}_{\rm gf}}{\delta\dot{\phi}^{i}\delta\dot{\phi}^{j}}=\frac{\delta^{2}S^{\lambda}_{\rm gf}}{\delta\dot{\varphi}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}\delta\dot{\varphi}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\,e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{j}}\equiv e^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{,{i}}G_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}e^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{,{j}}.

(4.16)

Then
\det F_{{i}{j}}
in the one-loop expressions (
2.49
) or (
II.4
) get replaced by

\det F^{i}_{\>{j}}\,=\,\det F^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\>{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\,\det\varTheta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\>{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}},

(4.17)

where raising the indices of operator
forms

F_{ij}
and
F_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
is done by the corresponding local metrics of Eq. (
4.14
) and the second index in the operator of the last determinant is analogously lowered by the “induced” metric
G_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
along normal directions,

F^{i}_{\>{j}}\equiv G^{{i}{k}}F_{{k}{j}},\,\quad\,F^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\>{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\equiv G^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}}F_{{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}},\,\quad\,\varTheta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\>{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\equiv\varTheta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{c}}}}G_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{c}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}.

(4.18)

Replacement of
F_{{i}{j}}
by (
4.17
) in (
2.49
) finally gives the one-loop generating functional of a restricted gauge theory fully in terms of the parental theory structures

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptZ^{1-\rm loop}_{\rm restricted}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\frac{\det\rho^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}}{\big(\!\det\varkappa^{\alpha\beta}\big)^{1/2}\big(\!\det\kappa_{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\big)^{1/2}}

(4.19)

\displaystyle\times\;\frac{\det F^{\alpha}_{\;\beta}}{\big(\!\det F^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\>{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\big)^{1/2}\big(\!\det\varTheta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\>{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\big)^{1/2}\det F^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\;{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}}\,.

Here the gauge-fixed ghost operator
F^{\alpha}_{\;\beta}
is, of course, given by the unreduced theory version of (
2.51
) with the projected generators
{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}=\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma}\,T^{\gamma}_{\>\beta}(Q,k)
(
3.20
),

F^{\alpha}_{\;\beta}\,=\,X^{\alpha}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma}\,T^{\gamma}_{\>\beta}-\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}{\rho^{-1}}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,\omega^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{\,\beta}.

(4.20)

Note that despite ultralocal reparametrization (
4.10
) the transition to parental theory representation leads to the additional nontrivial factor — the determinant of (
4.9
). The complexity of this factor follows from the fact that this is no longer a determinant of the local differential operator. Rather, this is the determinant of a
nonlocal
object
\varTheta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\>{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
— the Green’s function of the local operator
F_{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
sandwiched between two normal covariant vectors
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
and
\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
.

Ambiguity in the choice of generator bases

The BV generating functional (
2.10
) is not invariant under the change of generator bases of
\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
as obvious from one-loop expressions (
2.49
) or (
4.19
). When linearly transformed with respect to their gauge index
\alpha
gauge generators
\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}
still remain the generators of gauge transformations of the parental theory. A unique representations of
\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}
is not
a priori
fixed in Lagrangian theory. The linear transformations of
\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
with respect to its reducibility index
{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}
are also not fixed and this ambiguity reveals itself in the (yet) arbitrary matrix
\mu^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
(
3.27
)

\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,=\,k^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\,\mu^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}.

(4.21)

Ambiguity of the linear reparametrizations of generators results in “normalizing” factors in the generating functional and is the well-known feature of the BV formalism which will be addressed later in this section.

Another ambiguity is the choice of a projector parameter
k^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
which implicitly enters restricted theory gauge generators
\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}
(
3.20
) via projectors (
3.22
) and reducibility generators
\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\propto k^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
. The only restriction on the choice of
k^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
is that it should satisfy the rank conditions (
3.23
). Otherwise it is arbitrary, and this arbitrariness may extend to the effective action (
4.19
).
The problem of potential dependence of the latter on
k^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
is a specific issue of reducible gauge structure approach to restricted theory and it should be fixed independently of the generators normalization issue. Below we show that the requirement of independence of the generating functional (
4.19
) on the choice of
k^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
may be satisfied by a special choice of the factor
\mu^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
in (
4.21
).

IV.2
Independence of projector parameter

The variational equation

\displaystyle\!\!-\delta_{k}\varGamma^{1-\rm loop}=\,\delta_{k}\ln\frac{\det F^{\alpha}_{\;\beta}}{\det F^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\;{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}}

\displaystyle\quad=\,F^{-1\,\alpha}_{\;\;\;\;\,\,\,\beta}X^{\beta}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\,\delta_{k}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}-(\omega\mathcal{Z})^{-1\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\;\;\;\;\,\,\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,\omega^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{\,\beta}\delta_{k}\mathcal{Z}^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\,=\,0\qquad\quad

(4.22)

can be transformed by using the relation
\delta_{k}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}=\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\beta}\,\delta_{k}T^{\beta}_{\>\alpha}(Q,k)=\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\beta}\,\delta_{k}T^{\beta}_{\>\alpha}(Q,k)
(note that
\delta_{k}T^{\beta}_{\>\alpha}(Q,k)\propto T^{\beta}_{\>\gamma}(Q,k)
), the analog of the Ward identity (
2.62
) and the identity (
2.58
), so that

\displaystyle F^{-1\,\alpha}_{\;\;\;\;\;\;\,\gamma}\,(X^{\gamma}_{,{i}}\mathcal{R}^{i}_{\beta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F^{-1\,\alpha}_{\;\;\;\;\;\;\,\gamma}\big(F^{\gamma}_{\;\beta}+\sigma^{\gamma}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\,{\rho^{-1}}^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,\omega^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{\,\beta}\big)

(4.23)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}-\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}(\omega\mathcal{Z})^{-1\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\omega^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{\,\beta}.

For the first term in the right-hand side of (
IV.2
) this gives

F^{-1\,\alpha}_{\;\;\;\;\;\;\beta}\,X^{\beta}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\,\delta_{k}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}\,=\,(\omega\mathcal{Z})^{-1\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,\omega^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{\,\beta}\,T^{\beta}_{\>\alpha}(Q,k)\,\delta_{k}\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}},

(4.24)

where we used the fact that
\delta_{k}T^{\alpha}_{\>\alpha}=\delta_{k}(\delta^{\alpha}_{\,\alpha}-\delta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}})=0
and
\delta_{k}T^{\beta}_{\>\alpha}\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}=-T^{\beta}_{\>\alpha}\delta_{k}\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
in view of
T^{\beta}_{\>\alpha}(Q,k)\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}=0
. Thus Eq. (
IV.2
) takes the form

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt(\omega\mathcal{Z})^{-1\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,\omega^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{\,\beta}\,\big(T^{\beta}_{\>\alpha}(Q,k)-\delta^{\beta}_{\,\alpha}\big)\,\delta_{k}\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\qquad\,=\,(\omega\mathcal{Z})^{-1\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,(\omega k)^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{c}}}}\,(Qk)^{-1\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{c}}}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{d}}}}\,Q^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{d}}}}_{\,\alpha}\,\delta_{k}\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\,=\,0.\quad

(4.25)

With the expression (
4.21
) this equation reads in condensed matrix notations

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt{\rm tr}\,\big(\mu^{-1}\delta_{k}\mu+(Qk)^{-1}\delta_{k}(Qk)\big)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\qquad=\,\delta_{k}\ln\big(\det\mu\,\det(Qk)\big)\,=\,0,

(4.26)

where
{\rm tr}
denotes the trace over indices of the matrices
\mu=\mu^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
and
(Qk)=(Qk)^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
. Without loss of generality this equation can be solved by
\mu^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}=(Qk)^{-1\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
, so that finally

\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,=\,k^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\,(Qk)^{-1\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}_{\;\;\;\;\;\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}.

(4.27)

IV.3
Canonical normalization of generators

Specification of the gauge generators basis
\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}
is a more complicated issue. However, we will give brief and, perhaps, not so exhaustive arguments in favor of a concrete choice which, in particular, will confirm the form (
4.27
) of
\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
. Conventional Lagrangian quantization in the form of the Faddeev-Popov integral (or the BV integral in more complicated models with open and reducible algebras) is not intrinsically closed. It does not provide uniquely the concrete form of the local measure and does not resolve the associated problem of the choice of the generators bases. In order to fix them one should appeal to the canonical form of the path integral which for a general class of relativistic gauge conditions comprises the BFV formalism. For simple gauge systems
10
10

10

The simplification used here is that the Lagrangian gauge theory (which later will be the parental gauge system) admits the so-called one-step Hamiltonization. That is, when performing the Legendre transform only with respect to the fields with velocities in the Lagrangian action, one obtains the constrained canonical action having only first-class constraints, and Dirac consistency equations for these “primary” constraints do not generate new “secondary” constraints. This is the case of Einstein general relativity, Yang-Mills theory, and many others.

fixing the measure and the basis of generators looks as follows.

The starting point is the canonical formalism of the gauge theory whose canonical action
S_{\rm can}=\int dt\,(p\,\dot{q}\,-H-v^{\alpha}\gamma_{\alpha})
explicitly contains first-class constraints
\gamma_{\alpha}
dual to the Lagrange multipliers
v^{\alpha}
which are a part of the Lagrangian configuration space of the theory
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}=(q,v)
. This action is invariant with respect to gauge transformations which are canonical (ultralocal in time and generated by Poisson brackets with constraints) in the sector of phase space variables
\delta^{\xi}(q,p)=\{(q,p),\gamma_{\alpha}\}\xi^{\alpha}
, but contain the first-order time derivative of the gauge transformation parameter in the sector of Lagrange multipliers,
\delta^{\xi}v^{\alpha}=\dot{\xi}^{\alpha}+...\,
. Here dots denote ultralocal in time terms containing structure functions of the Poisson bracket algebra of first-class constraints and the Hamiltonian
H
, their explicit form being unimportant for us in what follows. The corresponding canonical path integral in the class of
canonical
gauges
\chi^{\alpha}=\chi^{\alpha}(q,p)
reads as

\displaystyle Z=\int Dq\,Dp\,Dv\,e^{\,i\,S_{\rm can}[q,p,v]}\prod_{t}\delta(\chi^{\alpha})\,\det\{\chi^{\alpha},\gamma_{\beta}\}

\displaystyle\quad=\int Dq\,Dp\,e^{\,i\int dt(p\dot{q}-H)}\prod_{t}\delta(\chi^{\alpha})\,\delta(\gamma_{\alpha})\det\{\chi^{\alpha},\gamma_{\beta}\},

and it is obviously invariant under the linear changes of the basis of constraints,
\gamma_{\alpha}\,\to\,\gamma^{\prime}_{\alpha}=\gamma_{\beta}\varOmega^{\beta}_{\,\alpha}
, with any ultralocal in time and nondegenerate matrix
\varOmega^{\beta}_{\,\alpha}
. Subsequent integration over momenta
p
converts this integral into the Lagrangian form which, modulo corrections associated with the transition to the Lagrangian expressions for momenta, takes the form of the Faddeev-Popov integral

Z\,=\int D\varphi\,\mu[\,\varphi\,]\,\det Q^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}\;\delta{(\chi^{\alpha})}\,e^{\,i\,S[\,\varphi\,]}.

(4.29)

Here the local measure
\mu[\,\varphi\,]\sim\exp\big[\delta(0)(...)\big]
absorbs ultralocal in time factors associated with the above corrections, and the Faddeev-Popov operator
Q^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}
is built in terms of the gauge generators
{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\beta}
— the Lagrangian version of the above transformations in the canonical formalism,
\delta^{\xi}\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}={\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}\xi^{\alpha}\propto\delta^{\xi}(q,v)
,

Q^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}\,=\,\chi^{\alpha}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\beta}.

(4.30)

Starting from the BFV canonical quantization
11
11

11

The Faddeev-Popov-type expression (
4.29
) is obtained by neglecting ghost vertices which contribute to higher loops and are irrelevant in the context of the present discussion. In the same manner we neglect possible higher-order loop contributions to the Lagrangian generating functional, which appear when the classical relation between momenta and velocities acquire ghost corrections.

one can arrive at the same expression for this wider class of gauge conditions
Henneaux:1992ig
;
FV:1975
including relativistic (or “dynamical”) gauges so that gauges
\chi^{\alpha}
involve linear dependence on the time derivatives of Lagrange multipliers
v^{\alpha}
.

The integral (
4.29
) is not explicitly invariant under the rotation of the generator basis,
{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}={\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\beta}\varOmega^{\beta}_{\,\alpha}
, but implicitly the choice of this basis is fixed by the requirement that it should match with the basis of canonical gauge transformations
\delta^{\xi}\varphi=\delta^{\xi}(q,v)
implying that
\delta^{\xi}v^{\alpha}=\dot{\xi}^{\alpha}+...
or
{\mathcal{R}}^{\alpha}_{\beta}=\delta^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}(d/dt)+...\,
. The ambiguity of splitting the configuration space of gauge fields
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}\sim(q,v)
into canonical coordinates and Lagrange multipliers (which is ultralocal in time) can only lead to some inessential local matrix
\mu^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}
in the last equation,
\delta^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}(d/dt)\to\mu^{\alpha}_{\beta}(d/dt)
, and some extra factor in the local measure
\mu[\,\varphi\,]\sim\exp\big[\delta(0)(...)\big]
which is again unimportant in concrete applications. Thus, the ambiguity in the choice of generator basis
{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\beta}
coming from the canonical formalism reduces to its rotation by ultralocal in time matrices, which is equivalent to changing the basis of first-class constraints in the canonical formalism. This ambiguity is physically inessential, so that finally the choice of generator basis is fixed by the requirement of local coefficient of the first-order time derivative in the gauge generators
{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}
.

Unfortunately, in the case of restricted gauge theories such a line of reasoning does not work directly because the reducible generators obtained by the projection procedure may become nonlocal in time. Moreover, the restriction of gauge theory generically leads to its canonical formalism with a much more complicated structure involving many generations of constraints. Therefore it is much harder to implement with the same level of generality the above scheme starting from the canonical quantization. For this reason we will choose a somewhat different approach in order to show that the equivalence to the canonical quantization fixes the basis of projected generators in restricted gauge theory by the requirement that the local parental theory generators
\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}
are canonically induced, that is they satisfy a local normalization condition for their time derivative part.

Consider a parental gauge theory with the action
\hat{S}[\,\varphi\,]
whose gauge generators
\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}
form a closed algebra and are
irreducible
, so that its generating functional is given by a standard Faddeev-Popov path integral. This integral can be represented on shell (with switched off sources) in three equivalent forms differing by the choice of gauge-fixing integration measure,

\displaystyle Z_{\rm parental}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int D\varphi\,\hat{M}^{\rm delta}_{(\chi)}\,e^{\,i\,\hat{S}[\,\varphi\,]}\,=\int D\varphi\,\hat{M}_{(\chi,\varkappa)}\,e^{\,i\,\hat{S}[\,\varphi\,]}

(4.31)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int D\varphi\,\hat{M}^{\rm delta}_{(\chi,\varkappa,\sigma)}\,e^{\,i\,\hat{S}[\,\varphi\,]}.

The measure factors
\hat{M}^{\rm delta}_{(\chi)}
and
\hat{M}_{(\chi,\varkappa)}
respectively correspond to the delta-function-type and Gaussian-type gauge fixing with the full set of gauge conditions
\chi^{\alpha}
, the latter involving the exponentiated gauge-breaking term with an invertible gauge-fixing matrix
\varkappa_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\hat{M}^{\rm delta}_{(\chi)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\det\hat{Q}^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}\,\delta{(\chi^{\alpha})},

(4.32)

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{(\chi,\varkappa)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\det\hat{Q}^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}\,(\det\varkappa_{\alpha\beta})^{1/2}\,e^{-i\,\frac{1}{2}\chi^{\alpha}\varkappa_{\alpha\beta}\chi^{\beta}}\!\!,\;\;\;

(4.33)

where
\hat{Q}^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}
is a standard Faddeev-Popov operator

\hat{Q}^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}\,=\,\chi^{\alpha}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\,\beta}.

(4.34)

Obviously,
\hat{M}^{\rm delta}_{(\chi)}=\hat{M}_{(\chi,\infty)}
is a limit of
\hat{M}_{(\varkappa)}
at
\varkappa_{\alpha\beta}\to\infty
.

The third measure factor is less known and corresponds to the situation when a part of gauge conditions
\chi^{p}
are of delta-function-type, whereas the rest of them are enforced via the gauge breaking term with the projector
\varPi_{\alpha\beta}

\hat{M}^{\rm delta}_{(\chi,\varkappa,\sigma)}\,=\,\frac{\det\hat{Q}^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}\,\big(\!\det\varkappa_{\alpha\beta}\big)^{1/2}}{\big(\!\det\kappa_{pq}\big)^{1/2}}\,e^{-\frac{i}{2}\chi^{\alpha}\varPi_{\alpha\beta}\chi^{\beta}}\delta(\sigma^{p}_{\,\alpha}\chi^{\alpha}).

(4.35)

Here

\sigma^{p}_{\,\alpha}\chi^{\alpha}\equiv\chi^{p}

(4.36)

is a subset of gauge conditions obtained by projecting the full subset
\chi^{\alpha}
with the aid of a vielbein
\sigma^{p}_{\alpha}=\kappa^{pq}\sigma^{\beta}_{\,q}\varkappa_{\beta\alpha}
which is dual to the set
\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,p}
introduced above in the formalism of reducible gauge generators,
12
12

12

The proof of the last equality in (
4.31
) can be done by complementing the bases of
\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,p}
with the remaining vielbein vectors
\sigma^{\alpha}_{M}
which are orthogonal to
\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,p}
in the metric
\varkappa_{\alpha\beta}
,
\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,p}\varkappa_{\alpha\beta}\sigma^{\beta}_{M}=0
and, therefore, satisfy the determinant relation
\det\,([\begin{array}[]{cc}\!\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,p}&\!\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,M}\end{array}\!])(\det\varkappa_{\alpha\beta})^{1/2}=(\det\kappa_{pq}\det\kappa_{MN})^{1/2}
, where
\kappa_{pq}=\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,p}\varkappa_{\alpha\beta}\sigma^{\beta}_{\,q}
and
\kappa_{MN}=\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,M}\varkappa_{\alpha\beta}\sigma^{\beta}_{\,N}
are respectively the metrics on subspaces spanned by
\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,p}
and
\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,M}
. Then the full delta function of gauge conditions can be decomposed as
\delta(\chi^{\alpha})=\delta(\chi^{p})\delta(\chi^{M})(\det\varkappa_{\alpha\beta})^{1/2}/(\det\kappa_{pq}\det\kappa_{MN})^{1/2}
and the factor
\delta(\chi^{M})/(\det\kappa_{MN})^{1/2}
here, according to ’t Hooft trick, being replaced by
\exp(-\frac{i}{2}\chi^{M}\kappa_{MN}\chi^{N})=\exp(-\frac{i}{2}\chi^{\alpha}\varPi_{\alpha\beta}\chi^{\beta})
— the implementation of the second equality of (
4.31
) in the sector of
\chi^{M}
gauges.
