\displaystyle\!\!\!\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\beta}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\beta,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\alpha}\,=\,(\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{{\,\delta}}-\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\,\delta},{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\gamma})T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+(\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma}T^{\gamma}_{\,\alpha,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{{\,\delta}}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}-\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\,\delta}}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\,\beta,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\gamma}T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}),

(B1)

and noting that in view of the open algebra relation for
\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\alpha}
the first group of terms here reads

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\big(\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{{\,\delta}}-\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\,\delta},{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\gamma}\big)T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\,\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\epsilon}\hat{C}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma{\,\delta}}T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}+\hat{E}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\gamma{\,\delta}}\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}.

(B2)

The second group of terms in the right-hand side of (
B
) requires differentiation of the projector
T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}
(
3.22
). This projector involves the procedure of inverting the matrix
(Qk)^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
whose rank is lower than the range of its indices and, therefore, requires the Moore-Penrose construction of the generalized matrix inversion
MoorePenrose
. This in turn leads to subtleties of variational procedure for
(Qk)^{-1\,a}_{\,\,\;\;\;\;b}
discussed in Appendix
A
. As shown there, the variational property of the projector is
effectively
equivalent to the naive use of the variational rule
\delta(Qk)^{-1}=-(Qk)^{-1}\delta(Qk)\,(Qk)^{-1}
, provided the rank restriction condition (
3.23
) holds, and it reads as (
A13
). This can be directly applied to the second group of terms in the right-hand side of (
B
) on using the symmetry
\theta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}=\theta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
, the algebra of parental theory generators, and their corollary

Q^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{\gamma,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{{\,\delta}}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}-(\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta)=\theta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}(\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\epsilon}\hat{C}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma{\,\delta}}T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}+\hat{E}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\gamma{\,\delta}}\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta})
.
As a result

\displaystyle\big(\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma}T^{\gamma}_{\,\alpha,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{{\,\delta}}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}-\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{{\,\delta}}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\,\beta,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\gamma}T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}\big)

\displaystyle\quad=-\big(\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma}T^{\gamma}_{\>\epsilon}k^{\epsilon}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}},{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}{(Qk)^{-1}}\vphantom{|}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}Q^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{\,\alpha}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{{\,\delta}}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}-(\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta)\big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\big(\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma}k^{\gamma}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}{(Qk)^{-1}}\vphantom{|}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}Q^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{\epsilon,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}T^{\epsilon}_{\>\alpha}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{{\,\delta}}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}-(\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta)\big)

\displaystyle\quad=-\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma}L^{\gamma}_{\,\epsilon}\hat{C}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma{\,\delta}}T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma}N^{\gamma}_{\,\alpha\beta}+\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma}N^{\gamma}_{\,\beta\alpha}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\epsilon}k^{\epsilon}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}{(Qk)^{-1}}\vphantom{|}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\theta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{,{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}}\hat{E}^{{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\gamma{\,\delta}}\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}.

(B3)

Here
N^{\gamma}_{\,\alpha\beta}
is defined by Eq. (
3.37
) and
L^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}
is the
longitudinal
projector complementary to
transverse
projector
T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}
,

L^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}\,\equiv\,\delta^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}-T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}\,=\,k^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}{(Qk)^{-1}}\vphantom{|}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}Q^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{\,\beta}.

(B4)

Therefore, summing the contributions (
B
) and (
B
) one observes that their first terms form out of
\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\epsilon}
the projected generator
\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\epsilon}
, so that we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\beta}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\beta,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\alpha}\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\epsilon}\hat{C}^{\epsilon}_{\gamma{\,\delta}}T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma}N^{\gamma}_{\,\alpha\beta}\!+\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma}N^{\gamma}_{\,\beta\alpha}

(B5)

\displaystyle+D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}}\hat{E}^{{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\gamma{\,\delta}}\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}

where
D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}}
is defined by Eq. (
3.38
),

\displaystyle D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}}\equiv\,\delta^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}}-\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\epsilon}k^{\epsilon}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}{(Qk)^{-1}}\vphantom{|}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\theta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{,{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}},

(B6)

and we used the fact that
\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\epsilon}=\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\beta}T^{\beta}_{\>\epsilon}
,
k^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}=L^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}k^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
and
Q^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{\,\gamma}=Q^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{\,\beta}L^{\beta}_{\>\gamma}
. Here the last term is not explicitly antisymmetric in indices
{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
and
{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}
. However,
due to the property
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}=\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
of the projector
D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
it can equivalently be rewritten in the antisymmetric form with

{E}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\alpha\beta}=D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}}D^{{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{L}{L}{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}{L}}}\hat{E}^{{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}{\mathchoice{L}{L}{\scriptscriptstyle{L}}{L}}}_{\gamma{\,\delta}}\,T^{\gamma}_{\>\alpha}T^{{\,\delta}}_{\>\beta}
.
This finally leads to the algebra (
3.34
) with structure functions (
3.35
)-(
3.38
).

The oblique projector
D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
defined by (
3.38
) enters the formalism when the parental algebra is open. Its left kernel is spanned by
{(Qk)^{-1}}\vphantom{|}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,\theta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
and the right kernel is spanned by the set of longitudinal gauge vectors
\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\beta}k^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}

\displaystyle D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}D^{{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}}\,=\,D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{K}{K}{\scriptscriptstyle{K}}{K}}},\,{(Qk)^{-1}}\vphantom{|}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\,\theta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\,=\,0,\quad

\displaystyle D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\beta}k^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\,=\,0.

(B7)

Moreover, this projector in the
\varphi^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}
-space converts the parental generator into the projected one in the space of gauge indices,
D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\beta}=\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha}T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}\equiv{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\beta}
, so that
\theta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\hat{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\beta}=0
.

When
\rank\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}=\rank Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}=m_{1}
then the second of relations (
B
) can be simplified to
\theta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}=0
. For rank-deficient
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}
, when
\rank\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}=m_{1}
and
\rank Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}=m_{1}-m_{2}
, the correct property is
\theta^{{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}=\big(1-(Qk)(Qk)^{-1}\big)\vphantom{|}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}\equiv{L_{1}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}\,\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}
, where
{L_{1}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}
is a projector on left zero vectors of
Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}
. For this generic (physically interesting case) the rank deficiency of
D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
equals the rank of gauge-restriction operator (
3.11
),
\corank D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}=\rank Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\beta}=\rank k^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
.

Note that the resulting open algebra of the restricted theory (
3.34
) closes on shell of the parental theory
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}=0
rather than on its own shell
\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}-\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}=0
. In view of the above relations one has

\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}=(\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}-\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}})D^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}}_{\,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}+\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{L_{1}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}
, so that the algebra (
3.34
) can be rewritten in the form

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\alpha,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\beta}-\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\beta,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}_{\alpha}\,=\,\mathcal{R}^{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}_{\gamma}C^{\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+{E}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\alpha\beta}\big(\hat{S}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}-\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}\big)+{E}^{{\mathchoice{I}{I}{\scriptscriptstyle{I}}{I}}{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}}_{\alpha\beta}\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{L_{1}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\theta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}_{,{\mathchoice{J}{J}{\scriptscriptstyle{J}}{J}}},

(B8)

where the last term, unless it is zero, breaks its closure on shell of the restricted theory. Note that
\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}{L_{1}}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}
is the part of the full set of Lagrange multipliers
\lambda_{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
which stay unrestricted by the equation of motion (
3.5
) for Lagrange multipliers.

Appendix C
Determinant relation

In this section we prove the determinant relation (
4.43
). All involved quantities are matrix (two-index) structures which allow us to omit indices implying standard matrix multiplication. There are indices of two types — lowercase Greek indices of some range
m_{0}
and lowercase Roman indices of the lower range
m_{1}<m_{0}
19
19

19

Regarding the rank of quantities with indices
{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}},..
see a brief discussion in the end of this section.
Quadratic
m_{0}\times m_{0}
matrices are denoted by capital Roman letters (e.g.
\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{T}\hskip-0.8pt
). The matrices mapping from
m_{0}
-dimensional space to a lower
m_{1}
-dimensional space and back will be either underlined or will respectively carry a line over them. Thus we have

\displaystyle\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\,\leftrightarrow\,\hat{Q}^{\alpha}_{\,\beta},\quad\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{I}\hskip-0.8pt\,\leftrightarrow\,\delta^{\alpha}_{\,\beta},\,

\displaystyle\underline{a}\,\leftrightarrow\,a^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\alpha},\quad\underline{\sigma}\,\leftrightarrow\,\sigma^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha},\quad\overline{\sigma}\,\leftrightarrow\,\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}},\quad\overline{k}\,\leftrightarrow\,k^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}},

\displaystyle\overline{\sigma}\,\underline{a}\,\leftrightarrow\,(\overline{\sigma}\,\underline{a})^{\alpha}_{\beta}\equiv\sigma^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}}a^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\beta},\quad\underline{\sigma}\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\,\overline{k}\,\leftrightarrow\,(\underline{\sigma}\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\overline{k})^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\equiv\sigma^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}\hat{Q}^{\alpha}_{\,\beta}k^{\beta}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}},

\displaystyle\underline{a}\,\overline{k}\,\leftrightarrow\,(\underline{a}\,\overline{k})^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}\equiv{a}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}{k}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}.

(C1)

Note that the matrix
\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt
here is a quadratic
m_{0}\times m_{0}
matrix and in Sec.
IV.3
it stands for the complete Faddeev-Popov operator for the rank-
m_{0}
parental gauge symmetry. We also assume that
\underline{\sigma}
and
\overline{\sigma}
form dual vectors in the sense that

\displaystyle\underline{\sigma}\,\overline{\sigma}=I\,\;\leftrightarrow\;\,\underline{\sigma}^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}\,\overline{\sigma}^{\alpha}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}=\delta^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{b}}}}.

(C2)

Then one can prove the following relation between the determinants of these matrices

\displaystyle\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{\det}\hskip-0.8pt\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt=\frac{\det(\underline{\sigma}\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\overline{k})}{\det(\underline{a}\overline{k})}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\;\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{\det}\hskip-0.8pt\,\Big(\underbrace{\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\,\big(\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{I}\hskip-0.8pt-\overline{k}\,(\underline{\sigma}\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\overline{k})^{-1}\underline{\sigma}\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\big)+\overline{\sigma}\,\underline{a}}_{\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{F}\hskip-0.8pt}\Big)\,.\quad

(C3)

It is also assumed that all determinants in (
C
) are nonzero.

Note that the
m_{0}\times m_{0}
matrix in the first determinant in the right-hand side of (
C
) can be written as

\displaystyle\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{F}\hskip-0.8pt=\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{T}\hskip-0.8pt+\overline{\sigma}\,\underline{a},\,\quad\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{T}\hskip-0.8pt=\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{I}\hskip-0.8pt-\overline{k}\,(\underline{\sigma}\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\overline{k})^{-1}\underline{\sigma}\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt,

(C4)

where
\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{T}\hskip-0.8pt\,\leftrightarrow\,T^{\alpha}_{\>\beta}(\underline{\sigma}\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt,k)
is in fact the oblique projector (
3.22
) with the left kernel
\underline{\sigma}\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\,\leftrightarrow\,\sigma^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\beta}\hat{Q}^{\beta}_{\,\alpha}=Q^{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}_{\,\alpha}
which can be identified with the gauge-restriction operator. The first term of the matrix
\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{F}\hskip-0.8pt
is therefore degenerate and has as right and left zero vectors
\overline{k}
and
\underline{\sigma}
respectively,
\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{T}\hskip-0.8pt\,\overline{k}=\underline{\sigma}\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{T}\hskip-0.8pt=0
. Therefore it can be interpreted as the analog of the Hessian of a gauge-invariant action, whereas the second term in
\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{F}\hskip-0.8pt
plays the role of a gauge-fixing term providing invertibility of this matrix.

The proof of the relation (
C
) can be done by using the basis in which the matrix
\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{F}\hskip-0.8pt
acquires a block-triangular form, but it is easier to use the analog of Ward identities in order to prove that the right-hand side is actually independent of the choice of arbitrary elements
\underline{\sigma}
,
\underline{a}
and
\overline{k}
and then check that this relation indeed holds under a special choice of these elements. Such a choice is obvious and reads as
\overline{\sigma}=\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\,\overline{k}
and
\underline{a}=\underline{\sigma}\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt
(when
\underline{\sigma}\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\,\overline{k}=\underline{a}\,\overline{k}=I
), so that it remains to check that the right-hand side is indeed
(\underline{\sigma},\underline{a},\overline{k})
-independent.

Multiplying the matrix (
C4
) from the left and from the right by zero vectors of its first term
\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{T}\hskip-0.8pt
one finds two Ward identities and their corollary,

\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{F}\hskip-0.8pt^{-1}\overline{\sigma}=\overline{k}\,(\underline{a}\overline{k})^{-1}\!,\;\;\,\underline{a}\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{F}\hskip-0.8pt^{-1}\!=\underline{\sigma},\;\;\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{F}\hskip-0.8pt^{-1}\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\,\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{T}\hskip-0.8pt=\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{I}\hskip-0.8pt-\overline{k}\,(\underline{a}\overline{k})^{-1}\underline{a}.

(C5)

Then, direct variation of the right-hand side of the relation (
C
) with respect to
\underline{a}
,
\overline{k}
and
\underline{\sigma}
shows that it is indeed independent of these quantities on account of the above identities and the variational version of duality relation (
C2
),
\underline{\sigma}\,\delta\overline{\sigma}+\delta\underline{\sigma}\,\overline{\sigma}=0
.

We finish this discussion with the note on applicability of the above relation to the case when the objects labeled by indices
{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
are rank-deficient (with the rank
m_{1}-m_{2}<m_{1}
). In Sec.
IV.3
, where this determinant relation was used, instead of indices
{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
we explicitly used the indices
p
belonging to the range
m_{1}-m_{2}
which symbolized the maximal rank (irreducible) representation. In such a representation all matrices are nondegenerate and quantities with mixed
indices are of the maximal rank. However the maximal rank
representation is not necessary and this proof runs equally well in
the reducible representation of rank-deficient objects
(\underline{\sigma},\overline{\sigma},\underline{a},\overline{k})
of rank
m_{1}-m_{2}
, provided the composite quantities
\overline{\sigma}\,\underline{a}
,
(\underline{a}\overline{k})
,
(\underline{\sigma}\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\overline{k})
have the same rank
m_{1}-m_{2}
20
20

20

This is the analog of the rank condition (
3.23
).
and the inverse degenerate matrices
(\underline{\sigma}\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\overline{k})^{-1}
,
(\underline{a}\overline{k})^{-1}
are treated in the Moore-Penrose sense (as was discussed in Appendix
A
).
Determinants of degenerate operators of rank
m_{1}-m_{2}
should be understood in an appropriate regularized sense, for example by omitting the contribution of zero eigenvalues in the eigenvector bases. If one goes into details of the variational proof, then the variations of regularized determinants turn out to be
\delta\det{(\underline{\sigma}\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\overline{k})}=\mathrm{tr}\,\big[\delta(\underline{\sigma}\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\overline{k})(\underline{\sigma}\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{Q}\hskip-0.8pt\overline{k})^{-1}\big]
with the Moore-Penrose definition of the inverse matrix. The uniqueness of
\hskip-0.8pt\mathbbmsl{T}\hskip-0.8pt
projector variation was shown in Appendix
A
. The uniqueness of the regularized determinant variation has a similar mechanism.

This line of reasoning justifies the validity of one-loop contributions to the effective action of the restricted theory of Secs.
III
and
IV.1
(even though in Sec.
II
these contributions were formally based on full-rank quantities with reducibility indices
{{\color[rgb]{0.28,0.02,0.03}{a}}}
).
